{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_9d09bd42da09464197af2c5f","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Runs exactly one test for the Lore test-command interface. The selector is\n// \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by lore-list-tests.mjs.\n// Jest's -t flag is a regex matched against the full name, so the name is\n// escaped and anchored - a name containing (parens) or {500} must match\n// literally - and the file is passed with --runTestsByPath, which takes a path\n// rather than the regex a bare positional argument would be.\n// A selector that matches nothing is a failure, never a silently green run of\n// an empty suite.\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport {readFileSync, rmSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {tmpdir} from 'node:os';\nimport {join} from 'node:path';\nimport process from 'node:process';\n\nconst selector = process.argv[2];\n\nif (!selector || !selector.includes('::')) {\n  process.stderr.write('usage: lore-run-test.mjs \"<file>::<full test name>\"\\n');\n  process.exit(2);\n}\n\nconst separator = selector.indexOf('::');\nconst file = selector.slice(0, separator);\nconst name = selector.slice(separator + 2);\nconst escaped = name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g, '\\\\$&');\n\nconst reportFile = join(tmpdir(), `hal-engine-jest-run-${process.pid}.json`);\n\nlet runFailed = false;\n\ntry {\n  execFileSync(\n    'npx',\n    [\n      'jest',\n      '--runTestsByPath',\n      file,\n      '-t',\n      `^${escaped}$`,\n      '--json',\n      `--outputFile=${reportFile}`,\n      '--coverage',\n      '--coverageReporters=lcov',\n    ],\n    {\n      cwd: process.cwd(),\n      stdio: ['ignore', 'inherit', 'inherit'],\n      // The suite runs as ESM; jest needs the flag in every spawn, not just npm test.\n      env: {...process.env, NODE_OPTIONS: `${process.env.NODE_OPTIONS ?? ''} --experimental-vm-modules`.trim()},\n    }\n  );\n} catch {\n  runFailed = true;\n}\n\nlet report;\n\ntry {\n  report = JSON.parse(readFileSync(reportFile, 'utf8'));\n} catch (error) {\n  process.stderr.write(`jest produced no readable report at ${reportFile}: ${error.message}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n} finally {\n  rmSync(reportFile, {force: true});\n}\n\n// Every test in the file that -t did not select is reported as pending, so the\n// count that matters is the ones actually executed.\nconst ran = report.numTotalTests - (report.numPendingTests ?? 0);\n\nif (ran === 0) {\n  process.stderr.write(`selector matched no test: ${selector}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n}\n\nprocess.exit(runFailed || report.numFailedTests > 0 ? 1 : 0);\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Emits the Lore test-command `list` shape: a single JSON array of\n// {id, name, file} objects, one per test, and nothing else on stdout.\n// Jest cannot resolve test names without executing them - there is no\n// dry-run - so the suite is run once with --json written to a temp file and\n// progress sent to stderr, keeping stdout pure.\n// The id is \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" - stable across runs,\n// and exactly what the run command's {selector} splits back apart.\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport {readFileSync, rmSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {tmpdir} from 'node:os';\nimport {join, relative} from 'node:path';\nimport process from 'node:process';\n\nconst reportFile = join(tmpdir(), `hal-engine-jest-report-${process.pid}.json`);\n\ntry {\n  execFileSync('npx', ['jest', '--json', `--outputFile=${reportFile}`], {\n    cwd: process.cwd(),\n    stdio: ['ignore', 'ignore', 'inherit'],\n    // The suite runs as ESM; jest needs the flag in every spawn, not just npm test.\n    env: {...process.env, NODE_OPTIONS: `${process.env.NODE_OPTIONS ?? ''} --experimental-vm-modules`.trim()},\n  });\n} catch {\n  // A failing test still produces a full report, and listing must not depend\n  // on the suite being green. An unreadable report below is the real failure.\n}\n\nlet report;\n\ntry {\n  report = JSON.parse(readFileSync(reportFile, 'utf8'));\n} catch (error) {\n  process.stderr.write(`jest produced no readable report at ${reportFile}: ${error.message}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n} finally {\n  rmSync(reportFile, {force: true});\n}\n\nconst tests = report.testResults.flatMap(suite => {\n  const file = relative(process.cwd(), suite.name);\n\n  return suite.assertionResults.map(assertion => ({\n    id: `${file}::${assertion.fullName}`,\n    name: assertion.fullName,\n    file,\n  }));\n});\n\nprocess.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(tests)}\\n`);","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"41f6f228-6d46-4bad-9bf2-e81e6c4a2827","uuid":"aea515ed-1941-4e58-8161-931d60c892fc","timestamp":"2026-10-30T16:26:24.632Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Runs exactly one test for the Lore test-command interface. The selector is\n// \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by lore-list-tests.mjs.\n// Jest's -t flag is a regex matched against the full name, so the name is\n// escaped and anchored - a name containing (parens) or {500} must match\n// literally - and the file is passed with --runTestsByPath, which takes a path\n// rather than the regex a bare positional argument would be.\n// A selector that matches nothing is a failure, never a silently green run of\n// an empty suite.\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport {readFileSync, rmSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {tmpdir} from 'node:os';\nimport {join} from 'node:path';\nimport process from 'node:process';\n\nconst selector = process.argv[2];\n\nif (!selector || !selector.includes('::')) {\n  process.stderr.write('usage: lore-run-test.mjs \"<file>::<full test name>\"\\n');\n  process.exit(2);\n}\n\nconst separator = selector.indexOf('::');\nconst file = selector.slice(0, separator);\nconst name = selector.slice(separator + 2);\nconst escaped = name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g, '\\\\$&');\n\nconst reportFile = join(tmpdir(), `hal-engine-jest-run-${process.pid}.json`);\n\nlet runFailed = false;\n\ntry {\n  execFileSync(\n    'npx',\n    [\n      'jest',\n      '--runTestsByPath',\n      file,\n      '-t',\n      `^${escaped}$`,\n      '--json',\n      `--outputFile=${reportFile}`,\n      '--coverage',\n      '--coverageReporters=lcov',\n    ],\n    {\n      cwd: process.cwd(),\n      stdio: ['ignore', 'inherit', 'inherit'],\n      // The suite runs as ESM; jest needs the flag in every spawn, not just npm test.\n      env: {...process.env, NODE_OPTIONS: `${process.env.NODE_OPTIONS ?? ''} --experimental-vm-modules`.trim()},\n    }\n  );\n} catch {\n  runFailed = true;\n}\n\nlet report;\n\ntry {\n  report = JSON.parse(readFileSync(reportFile, 'utf8'));\n} catch (error) {\n  process.stderr.write(`jest produced no readable report at ${reportFile}: ${error.message}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n} finally {\n  rmSync(reportFile, {force: true});\n}\n\n// Every test in the file that -t did not select is reported as pending, so the\n// count that matters is the ones actually executed.\nconst ran = report.numTotalTests - (report.numPendingTests ?? 0);\n\nif (ran === 0) {\n  process.stderr.write(`selector matched no test: ${selector}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n}\n\nprocess.exit(runFailed || report.numFailedTests > 0 ? 1 : 0);\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Emits the Lore test-command `list` shape: a single JSON array of\n// {id, name, file} objects, one per test, and nothing else on stdout.\n// Jest cannot resolve test names without executing them - there is no\n// dry-run - so the suite is run once with --json written to a temp file and\n// progress sent to stderr, keeping stdout pure.\n// The id is \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" - stable across runs,\n// and exactly what the run command's {selector} splits back apart.\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport {readFileSync, rmSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {tmpdir} from 'node:os';\nimport {join, relative} from 'node:path';\nimport process from 'node:process';\n\nconst reportFile = join(tmpdir(), `hal-engine-jest-report-${process.pid}.json`);\n\ntry {\n  execFileSync('npx', ['jest', '--json', `--outputFile=${reportFile}`], {\n    cwd: process.cwd(),\n    stdio: ['ignore', 'ignore', 'inherit'],\n    // The suite runs as ESM; jest needs the flag in every spawn, not just npm test.\n    env: {...process.env, NODE_OPTIONS: `${process.env.NODE_OPTIONS ?? ''} --experimental-vm-modules`.trim()},\n  });\n} catch {\n  // A failing test still produces a full report, and listing must not depend\n  // on the suite being green. An unreadable report below is the real failure.\n}\n\nlet report;\n\ntry {\n  report = JSON.parse(readFileSync(reportFile, 'utf8'));\n} catch (error) {\n  process.stderr.write(`jest produced no readable report at ${reportFile}: ${error.message}\\n`);\n  process.exit(1);\n} finally {\n  rmSync(reportFile, {force: true});\n}\n\nconst tests = report.testResults.flatMap(suite => {\n  const file = relative(process.cwd(), suite.name);\n\n  return suite.assertionResults.map(assertion => ({\n    id: `${file}::${assertion.fullName}`,\n    name: assertion.fullName,\n    file,\n  }));\n});\n\nprocess.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(tests)}\\n`);","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_9d09bd42da09464197af2c5f","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_8b72fb3790d84e9492e19b4c","type":"tool_result","content":"60\tand the changelog check.\n61\t\n62\tTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\n63\ttouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n64\t`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n65\t\n66\t## Spec Header Table\n67\t\n68\tEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\n69\tand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n70\t\n71\t```markdown\n72\t# <spec title>\n73\t\n74\t| Field  | Value                  |\n75\t| ------ | ---------------------- |\n76\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n77\t| Status | Draft                  |\n78\t\n79\t<lead paragraph>\n80\t```\n81\t\n82\tThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\n83\tcarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\n84\tthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\n85\tthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\n86\tanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n87\t\n88\t`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\n89\tthat predate this convention.\n90\t\n91\t`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\n92\tit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\n93\ttest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n94\t`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\n95\tbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\n96\tvalidate them.\n97\t\n98\tA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\n99\tparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\n100\tones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n101\t\n102\t| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n103\t| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n104\t| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n105\t| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n106\t| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n107\t| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n108\t| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n109\t\n110\tPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\n111\tfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\n112\tcoverage tier it will never earn.\n113\t\n114\tADRs do not carry this table. They declare their state as YAML frontmatter\n115\t`status:` instead - `accepted` for a decision the code implements, `proposed`\n116\tfor one it does not. That field does mean maturity, which the spec `Status` row\n117\texplicitly does not. The difference is deliberate: ADRs are exempt from the\n118\tcoverage tier, because asking a decision record to cite a test for every\n119\tstatement it makes is the wrong ask of a decision record.\n120\t\n121\t`.specify/spec.md` is exempt from this section. It is the repo-level system\n122\tspec, it predates the convention, and its content is deliberately frozen -\n123\tgiving it a header table and a lead paragraph would mean writing new prose into\n124\ta file that is meant to stay unchanged. The exemption covers the header table\n125\tand the lead paragraph only: the Lore coverage job does scan\n126\t`.specify/spec.md`, so link placement still applies to it.\n127\t\n128\t## Spec Test Links\n129\t\n130\tStatements in `specs/*/spec.md` cite their validating test in a trailing\n131\tparenthetical at the end of the statement, where a list item counts as one\n132\tstatement and a paragraph counts per sentence. Tests live beside their source\n133\tin this repo, so the path points into `src/`:\n134\t\n135\t```markdown\n136\t- All entries within a single assistant response are committed before the next\n137\t  user message is processed\n138\t  ([validated by: commits every entry before the next user message](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L42)).\n139\t```\n140\t\n141\tThe citation carries the test's **name** as well as its line. The name is what the\n142\tauthor cited; the line is a derived pointer, and it goes stale on its own - adding\n143\tan import above a suite moves every test below it, and the citation lands on a\n144\tdifferent, still-valid declaration without anything noticing. `npm run spec:names`\n145\tholds each citation to the test it names, and `npm run spec:names:fix` repoints the\n146\tline from the name. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job\n147\tboth index by it; a citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than\n148\tone test.\n149\t\n150\tA citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\n151\tanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\n152\tUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\n153\tconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\n154\tzero. A mid-statement link to something that is not a test file - a script, a\n155\tdoc, another spec - is ordinary prose, is never reported, and was never coverage\n156\tto begin with. A misplaced citation is fixed by moving it to the end of its\n157\tstatement, never by deleting it.\n158\t\n159\tIn a test file the `#Lnn` anchor must land on the `it(`, `test(` or `describe(`\n160\tdeclaration itself - not a blank line, not one holding only closing punctuation,\n161\tand not a line inside the test body. A citation onto an `expect` renders and\n162\tresolves like any other, and proves nothing: no reader can turn that line back\n163\tinto the name of a case. `scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` reports all four as\n164\t`rotten` and exits 1 in both modes. A citation into a file that is not a test -\n165\ta workflow, a config, `package.json` - has no declaration to land on and is held\n166\tonly to existing and carrying content.\n167\t\n168\tWhat no gate can catch is an anchor aimed at the wrong declaration: a real but\n169\tunintended `it(` passes every check. The tell is that the test the statement"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"41f6f228-6d46-4bad-9bf2-e81e6c4a2827","uuid":"6fb9067f-176e-46eb-9374-3d53e5ad8d8a","timestamp":"2026-10-30T16:26:38.616Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/b96e2fe87202/workspace/target/AGENTS.md","content":"and the changelog check.\n\nTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\ntouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n\n## Spec Header Table\n\nEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\nand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n\n```markdown\n# <spec title>\n\n| Field  | Value                  |\n| ------ | ---------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n| Status | Draft                  |\n\n<lead paragraph>\n```\n\nThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\ncarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\nthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\nthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\nanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n\n`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\nthat predate this convention.\n\n`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\nit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\ntest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\nbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\nvalidate them.\n\nA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\nparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\nones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n\n| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n\nPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\nfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\ncoverage tier it will never earn.\n\nADRs do not carry this table. They declare their state as YAML frontmatter\n`status:` instead - `accepted` for a decision the code implements, `proposed`\nfor one it does not. That field does mean maturity, which the spec `Status` row\nexplicitly does not. The difference is deliberate: ADRs are exempt from the\ncoverage tier, because asking a decision record to cite a test for every\nstatement it makes is the wrong ask of a decision record.\n\n`.specify/spec.md` is exempt from this section. It is the repo-level system\nspec, it predates the convention, and its content is deliberately frozen -\ngiving it a header table and a lead paragraph would mean writing new prose into\na file that is meant to stay unchanged. The exemption covers the header table\nand the lead paragraph only: the Lore coverage job does scan\n`.specify/spec.md`, so link placement still applies to it.\n\n## Spec Test Links\n\nStatements in `specs/*/spec.md` cite their validating test in a trailing\nparenthetical at the end of the statement, where a list item counts as one\nstatement and a paragraph counts per sentence. Tests live beside their source\nin this repo, so the path points into `src/`:\n\n```markdown\n- All entries within a single assistant response are committed before the next\n  user message is processed\n  ([validated by: commits every entry before the next user message](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L42)).\n```\n\nThe citation carries the test's **name** as well as its line. The name is what the\nauthor cited; the line is a derived pointer, and it goes stale on its own - adding\nan import above a suite moves every test below it, and the citation lands on a\ndifferent, still-valid declaration without anything noticing. `npm run spec:names`\nholds each citation to the test it names, and `npm run spec:names:fix` repoints the\nline from the name. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job\nboth index by it; a citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than\none test.\n\nA citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\nanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\nUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\nconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\nzero. A mid-statement link to something that is not a test file - a script, a\ndoc, another spec - is ordinary prose, is never reported, and was never coverage\nto begin with. A misplaced citation is fixed by moving it to the end of its\nstatement, never by deleting it.\n\nIn a test file the `#Lnn` anchor must land on the `it(`, `test(` or `describe(`\ndeclaration itself - not a blank line, not one holding only closing punctuation,\nand not a line inside the test body. A citation onto an `expect` renders and\nresolves like any other, and proves nothing: no reader can turn that line back\ninto the name of a case. `scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` reports all four as\n`rotten` and exits 1 in both modes. A citation into a file that is not a test -\na workflow, a config, `package.json` - has no declaration to land on and is held\nonly to existing and carrying content.\n\nWhat no gate can catch is an anchor aimed at the wrong declaration: a real but\nunintended `it(` passes every check. A\n22\t  // red run is fixed by adding the missing test, never by lowering the number\n23\t  // (specs/hal-engine-npm-release/spec.md).\n24\t  // The one exception: deleting covered code shrinks the denominator and lowers\n25\t  // the percentage while the uncovered count is unchanged. Compare covered/total\n26\t  // absolutes before re-baselining, and say so in the commit.\n27\t  // Per-path floors set at measurement, exactly as the global row is. An earlier version set them at\n28\t  // 70 with a comment claiming all four sat well above it - vertex's branches were 69.23, below it, and\n29\t  // four groups gained an 18-to-30 point regression budget the single global floor had never allowed.\n30\t  // `src/orchestration/tools/` is named separately because a path floor is a prefix match: without it a\n31\t  // regression in the tools registry hides inside the orchestration figure, which is what these exist\n32\t  // to prevent. Naming a path REMOVES its files from the `global` group, so the global numbers below\n33\t  // cover the remainder - the provider adapters and ws helpers with no suite - and are lower for that\n34\t  // reason rather than because coverage fell. Raise any of these with the measurement; never lower one\n35\t  // to go green (specs/hal-engine-npm-release/spec.md).\n36\t  // Only `branches` was re-measured on jest 30, which instruments branch points jest 29 never saw.\n37\t  // Across src/ the branch count went 240/350 covered to 332/473 while statements (665/842), functions\n38\t  // (180/226) and lines (601/756) are identical to the unit - the same 385 tests, none added, none\n39\t  // removed. No branch that was covered became uncovered, so a group whose percentage fell did so\n40\t  // because its denominator grew: vertex, mock and global rose, orchestration and its tools fell.\n41\t  // That is a finer instrument reading the same suite, which is why these two moved down.\n42\t  coverageThreshold: {\n43\t    'src/orchestration/': {statements: 88.3, branches: 80.85, functions: 92, lines: 87.91},\n44\t    'src/orchestration/tools/': {statements: 66.66, branches: 56.25, functions: 71.42, lines: 70.83},\n45\t    'src/infrastructure/stores/': {statements: 100, branches: 100, functions: 100, lines: 100},\n46\t    'src/providers/vertex/': {statements: 87.95, branches: 74.64, functions: 93.33, lines: 91.78},\n47\t    'src/providers/mock/': {statements: 100, branches: 78.57, functions: 100, lines: 100},\n48\t    global: {statements: 71.48, branches: 64.82, functions: 68.57, lines: 71.9},\n49\t  },\n50\t};\n51\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"41f6f228-6d46-4bad-9bf2-e81e6c4a2827","uuid":"9f157105-4f02-4599-8a72-5a9d2c7800e9","timestamp":"2026-10-30T16:26:46.589Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/b96e2fe87202/workspace/target/jest.config.js","content":"/** @type {import('ts-jest').JestConfigWithTsJest} */\nexport default {\n  testEnvironment: 'node',\n  roots: ['<rootDir>/src', '<rootDir>/scripts'],\n  testMatch: ['**/*.test.ts'],\n  // Fixture suites are inputs to the gate tests, not tests;
